Testimonies are all from White. He says: 0: looked at other window when heard thing fall. 1: light stand lying on floor when looked. 2: saw Miss Mia run to left. 3: killer attacked her but she dodged. 4: She turned and ran for the door. 5: Then you did her in with a single blow! Thwap!

For example, evidence 8 Floor Plans: "The stand is blocked from view looking from the office." That might contradict testimony 1: White saw light stand lying on floor. If floor plan says blocked from view, White couldn't see it. But White says he saw it. That is a contradiction.

Also evidence 5: Autopsy says cause: single blunt force trauma. White says "Then you did her in with a single blow! Thwap!" That matches. Not contradiction.

Evidence 9: May Testimony: "The victim dodged an attack then ran to the right, but she was caught and struck." White says she ran to left. Contradiction: direction. That is a spatial contradiction. Use rule: If XXX is at location YYY at time ZZZ then cannot be at different location at same time. But here it's about direction of run. White says left, May says right. So they contradict. But rule: "If XXX is at location YYY at time ZZZ then XXX cannot be at a different location at time ZZZ" could apply: if victim at left vs right at same time. So we can use that rule.

Thus pair: evidence 9 (May Testimony) and testimony 2 or 4? Actually testimony 2 says she ran to left. May Testimony says ran to right. So evidence 9 and testimony 2 or 4. But we need pair of evidence and testimony. So evidence 9 and testimony 2 (or 4). Let's pick testimony 2: "Then, the next moment, I saw Miss Mia run to the left!" That contradicts May Testimony. So pair evidence 9, testimony 2.

Check rule: "If XXX is at location YYY at time ZZZ then XXX cannot be at a different location at time ZZZ" applies: victim cannot be at left and right simultaneously. So contradiction.

Thus output JSON: {"evidence": 9, "testimony": 2}.
